14.2. In Vivo Studies
A Placebo-Controlled, Dose-Escalation Study to Assess the Safety, Tolerability, 
Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamics of Single and Multiple Doses of ISIS 678354, a 
GalNAc3 Conjugated 2ʹ-MOE Chimeric Antisense Oligonucleotide Targeting ApoC-III, 
Administered Subcutaneously to Healthy Volunteers with Elevated Triglycerides (Trial 
ISIS 678354–CS1)
Objectives
The objectives of the study were to evaluate the safety and tolerability, PK, and effects on 
pharmacodynamics (PD), including plasma ApoC-III, TG, total cholesterol (TC), low-density 
lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C), high-density lipoprotein cholesterol (HDL-C), non-HDL-C, and 
very low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (VLDL-C), of single and multiple SC doses (both 
weekly and Q4W dosing regimens) of olezarsen in healthy volunteers with elevated TG.
Study Design
This was a Phase 1, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, single-center, dose-escalation 
study. There were five ascending single-dose cohorts (N=8 per cohort, total randomized 6 
active:2 placebo) with olezarsen doses of 10, 30, 60, 90, and 120 mg. The posttreatment 
evaluation period was Day 2 through Day 150 for the single-dose cohorts.
Volunteers in the multiple-dose cohorts were evaluated on weekly dosing for 6 weeks and Q4W 
for 3 months. The olezarsen doses were 15 mg (Cohort AA) and 30 mg (Cohort BB) for the 
weekly dosing cohorts (N=8 per cohort, total randomized 6 active:2 placebo) and 60 mg for the 
Q4W dosing cohort (Cohort CC; N=10, total randomized 6 active:4 placebo). The post-treatment 
evaluation period was Day 37 through Day 155 for the weekly multiple-dose cohorts and Day 86 
through Day 204 for the Q4W multiple-dose cohort.
Results
PK
Following SC administration, olezarsen was rapidly absorbed into the systemic circulation, with 
median Tmax ranging from 1 to 3 hours. Plasma olezarsen concentrations then declined over time 
in a multiphasic manner, with an initial rapid distribution phase dominating the plasma clearance 
followed by a much slower elimination phase with apparent terminal half-life ranging from 3 to 
6 weeks (Figure 44). Plasma Cmax and area under the concentration-time curve (AUC) increased 
slightly greater than dose proportionally over the dose range of 10 to 120 mg of olezarsen. No 
increase in Cmax or AUC was observed after repeated weekly or Q4W administration (Figure 45). 
These data support time-invariant plasma kinetics for olezarsen.
Rapid distributional clearance from plasma and limited recovery of full-length ASO in urine 
(<1% of administered dose over a 24-hour period) suggests that clearance from plasma occurred 
primarily by distribution to tissues, where the drug is metabolized to chain-shortened 
oligonucleotide metabolites prior to renal excretion.
Reference ID: 5499859

PD
PD results showed dose-dependent sustained reductions in fasting plasma ApoC-III and TG 
levels, with particularly marked, statistically significant, reductions across the higher single-dose 
and all multiple-dose cohorts. Among the single-dose cohorts, reductions of 12%, 11%, 43%, 
68%, and 77% (TG) and 4%, 32%, 65%, 78%, and 91% (ApoC-III) for the 10, 30, 60, 90, and 
120 mg groups, respectively, were seen at Day 15, 2 weeks after dosing (Figure 46). The effects 
were sustained at Day 30, with reductions of 38%, 60%, and 72% (TG) and 58%, 72%, and 87% 
(ApoC-III) for the 60, 90, and 120 mg groups, respectively.

Among the weekly multiple-dose cohorts, reductions of 61% and 71% (TG) and 65% and 84% 
(ApoC-III) for the 15 and 30 mg groups were seen at Day 43 (one week after the last dose),

(TG) and 63% and 79% (ApoC-III) for the 15 and 30 mg groups. Similarly, increases in HDL-C 
concentrations of 50% to 56% from baseline were observed at Day 43 in the 15 and 30 mg 
weekly multiple-dose groups, respectively.

A Randomized Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Non-Confirmatory Study to Assess 
Safety, Tolerability, PK, and PD of Single Ascending and Multiple Doses of ISIS 678354 in 
Healthy Japanese Participants (Trial AKCEA-CS1)
Objectives
The objectives of the study were to assess the safety and tolerability (primary), PK (secondary), 
effects on fasting serum TGs and ApoC-III levels (exploratory), and immunogenicity of single 
and multiple SC dose administration of olezarsen in healthy Japanese participants.
Study Design
This was a randomized, placebo-controlled, participant and investigator-blinded, single 
ascending dose and multiple-dose study in healthy Japanese male and female participants 
conducted in the USA. In the single ascending dose portion, participants were randomized to 
olezarsen or placebo at each of three serial dose levels (30, 60, or 90 mg). For the multiple-dose 
part of this study, 60 mg of olezarsen or placebo was administered SC Q4W on Days 1, 29, 57, 
and 85.
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 245 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
228
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Results
PK
Following single- or multiple-dose SC administration in healthy Japanese participants, olezarsen 
was absorbed rapidly into the systemic circulation, with median Tmax ranging from 2 to 3 hours. 
After reaching Cmax, plasma concentrations of olezarsen declined in a multi-phasic fashion, with 
a rapid disposition phase that dominated the plasma clearance, followed by a slower elimination 
phase with terminal elimination half-life of 8.2 to 27.5 days for single-dose cohorts and 30.8 
days for the multiple-dose cohort. Of note, the half-life of 8.2 days observed following a single 
30-mg dose of olezarsen is likely underestimated due to a majority of samples in the elimination 
phase being below the LLOQ. Plasma Cmax and AUC increased slightly greater than dose 
proportionally over a dose range of 30 to 90 mg. There was no increase in plasma Cmax or AUC 
after repeated Q4W administration of 60 mg olezarsen for 13 weeks.
The fraction of olezarsen excreted over 24 hours ranged from 0.0264% to 0.0741%, suggesting 
negligible renal elimination. Renal clearance over the first 24 hours also increased with dose and 
ranged from 6.24 mL/h to 12.0 mL/h.
PD
Following Single SC Administration of 30 mg, 60 mg, or 90 mg ISIS 678354
Following single SC doses of olezarsen at 30 mg, 60 mg, and 90 mg, mean percent decreases in 
TG levels from baseline were observed beginning on Day 3 and continued through Day 120. The 
most considerable effect of the administration of olezarsen on plasma TG levels compared with 
placebo appeared to occur on Day 15 postdose; the percentage differences (p-values) were 
-62.36% (0.124), -97.24% (0.021), and -83.78 (0.046) for the 30 mg, 60 mg, and 90 mg 
treatment groups, respectively. The percentage change in TG levels from baseline does not 
appear to be dose-dependent; the effect on TG levels appeared similar following single 
administration of either 60 mg or 90 mg ISIS 678354, and smaller following administration of 30 
mg ISIS 678354.
Significant percentage decreases in ApoC-III levels following administration of ISIS 678354 
compared with placebo were observed beginning on Day 8 in the 60 mg and 90 mg treatment 
groups and beginning on Day 15 in the 30 mg treatment group, with percentage differences (p-
values) -61.02% (0.0004), -64.77% (0.0002), and -39.40 (0.0103), respectively. Significant 
decreases in ApoC-III levels continued to Day 90 postdose in the 60 mg and 90 mg treatment 
groups, and to Day 30 postdose in the 30 mg treatment group, with percentage differences (p-
values) -39.84% (0.0023), -22.35% (0.0303), and -41.09 (0.0092), respectively. The most 
considerable effect of the administration of 30 mg, 60 mg, and 90 mg ISIS 678354 on plasma 
ApoC-III levels compared with placebo appeared to occur on Day 30, Day 8, and Day 15 
postdose, respectively.
Following Multiple SC Administration of 60 mg ISIS 678354 Every 4 Weeks for a Total of Four 
Doses (Day 1, Day 29, Day 57, Day 85)
Following four SC doses (Day 1, Day 29, Day 57, and Day 85) of 60 mg olezarsen Q4W, mean 
percent decreases from baseline in TG levels were consistently observed from Day 2 to Day 204, 
119 days after the last dose. The percentage change in TG from baseline ranged from -73.8% on 
Reference ID: 5499859

NDA 218614

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Day 92 to -31.7% on Day 204. The most considerable effect of the administration of ISIS 
678354 on plasma TG levels compared with placebo appeared to occur on Day 113 where the 
percentage difference (p-value) was -71.96% (0.004). 
Percentage decreases in ApoC-III levels from baseline results and differences in ApoC-III levels 
compared with placebo were consistently decreased from Day 2 to Day 204. The percentage 
change in ApoC-III from baseline ranged from -81.6% on Day 92 to -26.3% on Day 204. The 
most considerable effect of the administration of ISIS 678354 on plasma ApoC-III levels 
compared with placebo appeared to occur on Day 92 postdose where the percentage difference 
(p-value) was -81.63% (0.0002).
Immunogenicity
In single-dose cohorts, all participants who received placebo treatment had negative ADA results 
during the study. Overall, in the single-dose cohorts, three (15.0%) participants were assessed as 
ADA positive during the study (two [40.0%] participants in the 60 mg ISIS 678354 treatment 
group and one [16.7%] participant in the 90 mg ISIS 678354 treatment group). There was no 
association or trend between the incidence of ADA and dose level. 
In multiple-dose cohorts, two (100%) participants who received placebo treatment had positive 
ADA results at Day 1 (titer of 50 for both participants), and three (50.0%) participants in the 60 
mg ISIS 678354 treatment group were assessed as ADA positive at postdose time points during 
the study. There was a trend of increased ADA incidence with increased number of doses. No 
participants who tested ADA positive reported any TEAEs during the study.
A Single-Dose, Randomized, Open-Label, Two-Period Crossover Bioequivalence Study 
Comparing Two Subcutaneous Formulations: Vial and Autoinjector With Olezarsen, at 
Two Dose Levels, in Healthy Adult Participants (Trial ISIS 678354-CS20)
Objectives
Primary Objective
The primary objective of this study was to assess the bioequivalence of olezarsen between two 
SC formulations at two dose levels: (1) autoinjector (
 mg/mL) and vial (100 mg/mL) at the 
50 mg dose; and (2) autoinjector (100 mg/mL) and vial (100 mg/mL) at the 80 mg dose, in 
healthy adult participants.
The primary endpoint was to assess whether the bioequivalence at each dose level calculated at 
the 90% CI for the ratio of geometric means (GMs) for AUC0-336h and Cmax for olezarsen fell 
within 80.00% to 125.00%.
Exploratory Objective
The exploratory objective of this study was to assess serum ApoC-III reduction from baseline 
over time (pre-dose through 91 days post-last dose).
Study Design
This was a Phase 1, single-dose, randomized, open-label, 2-period, 2-sequence, 2-treatment, 2-
way crossover, BE study that evaluated two dose levels of olezarsen (50 mg and 80 mg). 
Reference ID: 5499859
(b) (4)

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Following an up to 28-day screening period, 104 eligible participants were randomized to two 
dose groups (50 mg or 80 mg). There was a 28- to 42-day washout period between Day 1 of 
treatment period 1 and Day 1 of treatment period 2 to avoid PK carry over effects of the 
preceding treatment. Blood samples for PK analyses were collected at pre-dose (0 hour) and at 
0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 12, 24, 36, 48, 96, 168, and 336 hours postdose in each of the two 
treatment periods and during the post-treatment follow-up period (28, 56, and 91 days post-last 
dose). Blood samples for PD analysis (i.e., serum ApoC-III and serum TGs) were collected 
during treatment periods 1 and 2 on Day -1, Day 1 (pre-dose), Day 8, and Day 15 and the post-
treatment follow-up period (28, 56, and 91 days post-last dose).

Conclusions
•
The primary endpoint of BE between the autoinjector and the vial presentations based on 
AUC0-336h was achieved for both olezarsen 50 mg and 80 mg dose levels. 
•
Bioequivalence between the autoinjector and the vial presentations based on Cmax was 
achieved for the olezarsen 50 mg dose level. For the olezarsen 80 mg dose level, the 
geometric mean ratio 90% CI for Cmax was slightly above the standard upper CI limit for BE 
(80.00% to 125.00%), by 4.99%; however, this small difference is not clinically relevant due 
to the short duration around Cmax during which plasma concentration versus time profiles 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 252 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
235
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
showed a small difference, and the rest of the concentration-time profiles were nearly 
superimposable.
•
The PD effect of olezarsen on ApoC-III and TG reduction was similar between autoinjector 
and vial presentations at either the olezarsen 50 mg or 80 mg dose, which also supports that 
the small difference in Cmax at the olezarsen 80 mg dose is not clinically relevant.
•
There were no clinically meaningful differences in safety profile and ISRs between the 
autoinjector and vial presentations for olezarsen at either olezarsen 50 mg or 80 mg dose.
14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and 
Performance
Quantification of Olezarsen Concentrations in Human Plasma
Briefly, olezarsen was quantitatively measured directly from human plasma using a hybridization 
ECL assay. In this assay, samples were treated with proteinase K to mitigate potential 
interference from ADAs. Olezarsen in samples was hybridized to a complementary capture 
probe, which was biotinylated on the 5′-end. The hybridized olezarsen was then immobilized on 
a streptavidin-coated MesoScale Discovery (MSD) plate. A detection probe labeled with 
ruthenium (MSD-Tag) on the 3′-end is added to the plate to bind to the free portion of olezarsen. 
After addition of read buffer, the plate was read in an MSD Sector PRTM 600 plate reader.
The method validation evaluated the quantification range, accuracy, precision, potential 
interference, and cross-reactivity to selected putative metabolites, hook effect, dilutional 
linearity, selectivity, matrix effect, hemolysis and lipemia effects, potential ADA interference, 
quantification of unconjugated olezarsen (ISIS 304801, volanesorsen) and total full-length ASO, 
and stability. The validation range was 0.015 nM (0.13 ng/mL) to 20 nM (173.7 ng/mL), with the 
low end of the range defining the LLOQ.
Validation results demonstrated that the method was suitable for quantification of olezarsen and 
unconjugated olezarsen. The method performance over the validation is provided in Table 114.

Quantification of Olezarsen Concentrations in Human Urine
Olezarsen in human urine was quantitatively measured using a partially validated hybridization 
ELISA. Olezarsen was hybridized to a complementary cutting probe (678354LNARP), which 
was biotinylated at the 5′-end of the sequence and had digoxigenin at the 3′-end. The hybridized 
solution was then placed in a NeutrAvidin-coated ELISA plate. After binding had taken place, 
S1 nuclease was added to digest any unhybridized probes. An anti-digoxigenin antibody 
conjugated to alkaline phosphatase was used to detect the hybridized analyte by binding to the 
digoxigenin label. AttoPhos was used as a substrate for fluorometric readout. Fluorescence 
intensity was determined using a fluorescent plate reader. The hybridization ELISA is capable of 
quantifying olezarsen and unconjugated olezarsen (ISIS 304801) equivalently; therefore, the 
assay is capable of quantifying total full-length ASOs of olezarsen, using olezarsen for 
calibration. The method performance over the validation is provided in Table 115.

14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of 
PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
A validated qualitative ELISA method was used for the detection and assessment of anti-
olezarsen antibodies in human plasma in a multi-tiered approach: (1) screening assay, which 
identifies initial positive or negative samples; (2) confirmation assay, which assesses specificity 
of the positive screen samples by free-drug competition; and (3) titration assay, which estimates 
the level of antibody for the confirmed positive samples. Assay sensitivity was determined using 
affinity purified rabbit anti-olezarsen IgG and was defined as 9.65 ng/mL and 39.1 ng/mL in neat 
human plasma for the screening and confirmatory assays, respectively. The assay’s ability to 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 256 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
239
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
detect anti-olezarsen antibodies in the presence of circulating olezarsen (drug tolerance) was 
determined to be 39.1 ng/mL of antibody in the presence of up to 4000 ng/mL of olezarsen. No 
neutralizing assay was developed.
ADA sampling was obtained from patients with FCS from Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 on Days 1, 15, 
29, 57, 85, 169, 253, 365, and follow-up visit. Treatment-emergent (treatment-induced and 
treatment-boosted) ADAs against olezarsen were detected in 38.1% (8/21) of patients in the 50 
mg olezarsen group, 45.5% (10/22) of patients in the 80 mg olezarsen group, and 0% (0/23) of 
patients who received placebo. As a reference, the proportion of patients with ADAs detected at 
baseline were 38.1% (8/21), 27.3% (6/22), and 43.5% (10/23) of patients in the 50 mg olezarsen, 
80 mg olezarsen, and pooled placebo groups, respectively; an overall baseline positive rate of 
36.4% (24/66). Of note, 26 out of the 66 patients in the study had previous treatment with 
volanesorsen. The baseline positive rate was 53.8% (14/26) and 25.0% (10/40) in patients with 
and without previous volanesorsen treatment, respectively.
Impact of Immunogenicity on Pharmacokinetics
Higher plasma olezarsen Ctrough levels were consistently observed in ADA-positive patients 
(either treatment-unaffected or treatment-emergent ADA) compared with ADA-negative patients 
across olezarsen clinical studies. In Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 specifically, mean trough plasma 
olezarsen concentrations in the olezarsen 80 mg group were elevated up to approximately 10-
fold in FCS patients with treatment-unaffected and treatment-emergent ADA relative to ADA-
negative patients, and the corresponding elevations for patients in the olezarsen 50 mg group 
were up to approximately 5-fold relative to ADA-negative patients.
The mean (+SD) olezarsen trough plasma concentrations over time stratified by ADA category 
in patients with FCS in Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 are presented in Figure 51.

severity, or their relationship to treatment, nor are there any such trends for serious TEAEs.

bleeding, thrombocytopenia, renal, or hepatic TEAEs and ADA status. With respect to 
hypersensitivity OAEIs, there was no obvious relationship between hypersensitivity reactions

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
The population PK, exposure-ApoC-III, and exposure-TG modeling was considered reasonable 
for describing olezarsen, ApoC-III, and TG concentrations following a single or multiple dose 
administration of olezarsen and for performing PK and PD simulations to predict steady-state 
olezarsen exposure metrics and the effect on ApoC-III and TG levels. The PK and PD modelling 
were utilized to support the current submission as outlined below.
Table 116. Utility of the Population PK Modeling

Reviewer’s Comments
• The PK model included weight as a covariate on 
CL/F, Vc/F, Q/F, Vp/F, and ka. Japanese 
participants had 29% higher Vc/F. Both ka and 
relative F were higher for the autoinjector 
compared to the vial by 13% and 5%, 
respectively. ADA positive status with ADA titer of 
100 was associated with 30% decrease in CL/F.
• The model-predicted PK was used to develop 2 
independent exposure-response models, 
describing the relationship between of olezarsen 
concentrations and either apoC-III or triglyceride 
(TG) concentrations.
• The exposure-apoC-III model estimated an IC50 
(concentration at half maximum inhibition effect 
on apoC-III production rate) of 0.44 ng/mL, in 
patients with FCS. The Applicant’s model 
estimated a 30% increase in IC50 in ADA positive 
subjects with an ADA titer of 50. The exposure-
TG model estimated an EC50 (concentration at 
half maximum effect on TG elimination rate 
constant) of 1.48 ng/mL, in patients with FCS. 
The Applicant’s model estimated an increase in 
EC50 with an ADA titer above 50.
• The PK and exposure-response models were 
used to perform population simulations for 
olezarsen 50 mg or 80 mg, administered every 28 
days (Q4W) or 30 days (Q1M), to support dose 
selection in patients with FCS. The Applicant’s 
simulations indicated that the PK, apoC-III, and 
TG profiles for Q4W and Q1M dosing overlap 
following first dose and at steady-state with only 
marginal difference in median olezarsen 
concentrations, percentage change from baseline 
in apoC-III and TG for the respective doses and 
dosing regimens.

predicting median and individual observed 
concentrations of olezarsen and their variability 
in healthy volunteers, patients with FCS and 
patients with HTG and CVD.

expected to increase Ctrough,ss and AUC,ss, the

adequately describes the median and individual

with FCS and patients with HTG and CVD.

unexplored covariate and looks driven only by

affect the PD model parameters, except for the 
effect of FCS disease on IC50, suggesting that 
FCS patients are estimated to IC50 of 0.63 
ng/mL).

14.5.1. Applicant’s PK Analysis
The Applicant developed a population PK model for olezarsen to characterize the PK properties 
of olezarsen administered SC. The data for PK modeling were collected from three Phase 1 
studies in healthy subjects (ISIS 678354-CS1, ISIS 678354-CS20, and AKCEA-CS1), one Phase 
2 study (ISIS 678354-CS2) in patients with HTG and established CVD or at high risk for CVD, 
and two Phase 3 studies (ISIS 678354-CS3 and -CS8) in patients with FCS and in patients with 
CVD and HTG, respectively (Table 117). 
The PK dataset consisted of 6535 quantifiable PK samples from 415 subjects. Concentrations 
below the limit of quantification (n=419, <0.13 ng/mL) were excluded from the analysis dataset 
as they represented 6% (less than 10%) of all the data.
Table 118 summarizes the demographic characteristics of subjects included in the PK analyses, 
stratified by study. Table 119 summarizes the ADA titers by study. Most patients (85%, n=122) 
had ADA titers between 50 and 800, 12% of patients (n=17) had ADA titers between 1600 and 
3200, 3.4% of patients (n=5) had ADA titers between 6400 and 25600, among whom one patient 
had a single transient peak ADA at 51200.

Final Population PK Model
The PK model for olezarsen consisted of a two-compartment disposition model with first-order 
absorption and a linear elimination from the central compartment. Between subject variability 
(BSV) terms were included on apparent clearance (CL/F), apparent central volume (Vc/F), 
apparent inter-compartmental clearance (Q/F), apparent peripheral volume (Vp/F), and the 
absorption rate (ka). Between-occasion variability was included on CL/F and Vp/F, where 
occasion is the time-varying ADA positivity status (binary variable, with ADA positive status 
defined from time of first ADA appearance). The proportional residual unexplained variability 
(RUV) was implemented as an additive residual error on the log-scale. The parameter estimates 
from the final octreotide PK model are listed in Table 120.
Weight was included as a covariate on CL/F, Vc/F, Q/F, Vp/F, and ka. Japanese participants (i.e., 
Trial AKCEA-CS1) had 29% higher Vc/F compared to the other ethnicities across the studies. 
Both ka and relative F were higher for the autoinjector compared to the vial by 13% and 5%, 
respectively. ADA-positive status with ADA titer of 100 was associated with 30% decrease in 
CL/F.
The goodness of fit (GOF) plots from the final PK model are shown in Figure 55 and the 
prediction-corrected visual predict check (pcVPC) plots are shown in Figure 56.
Reviewer Comment and Assessment of the Population PK Model
Overall, the Applicant’s PK model is acceptable and adequately describes the individual 
observed concentrations of olezarsen and their variability in healthy volunteers, patients with 
FCS and patients with HTG and CVD. 
•
The PK parameters from the PK model were estimated with an acceptable precision (<23% 
relative standard error [RSE]). The estimated interindividual random error (eta-) shrinkage 
was low (<30%) and acceptable for the reliable graphical assessment of covariate effects, 
except for ka (eta-shrinkage =55%). The residual error (epsilon-) shrinkage was low (9%), 
indicating the informativeness of the GOF plots to diagnose structural and residual error 
model misspecifications. The GOF plots show that the PK model fitted the observed data 
well with no critical trends or bias in the predicted versus the observed concentrations of 
olezarsen and in the conditional weighted residuals (CWRES) versus predictions or time 
from all studies and olezarsen dose levels. 
•
The pcVPC plots, stratified by ADA status, indicate that the Applicant’s PK model 
reasonably captures and describes the observed median olezarsen concentrations and their 
variability form all studies with minimal bias.
•
ADA titer was found to be the most influential covariate on CL/F, AUC and Ctrough. Figure 
57 summarizes the predicted effect of ADA titer on the steady-state exposure metrics 
(AUCss, Cmax,ss and Ctrough,ss).

14.5.2. Applicant’s Exposure-Response Analyses
The Applicant used the model-predicted PK to develop two exposure-response models, using 
sequential PK-PD modeling. The exposure-response models described the relationship between 
of olezarsen concentrations and either ApoC-III concentrations or TG concentrations. 
The exposure-ApoC-III dataset consisted of 4518 quantifiable ApoC-III concentrations paired 
with PK information from 415 subjects. ApoC-III concentrations below the limit of 
quantification (n=196, <0.94 mg/dL) were excluded from the analysis dataset as they represented 
less than 10% of all the data. The exposure-TG dataset consisted of 4728 TG concentrations 
paired with PK information from 415 subjects. No trends in ApoC-III or TG levels over time 
were observed for placebo subjects, hence, placebo subjects were excluded from the analyses.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
14.5.2.1. Exposure-ApoC-III Model
The exposure-ApoC-III model consisted of an indirect response model with an inhibitory drug 
effect on ApoC-III production rate (Kin). The drug effect on Kin was described as an inhibitory 
Emax function with maximum inhibition (Imax) and a concentration at half maximum inhibition 
(IC50). BSV terms were included on the ApoC-III removal rate constant (Kout) and IC50 
parameters. The RUV in ApoC-III concentrations was implemented using a proportional residual 
error model, with separate RUV estimated by ADA status.
Table 121 summarizes the parameter estimates of the final exposure-ApoC-III model. The model 
identified disease status as a covariate on Kout and IC50, with FCS patients having 51% lower Kout 
and 80% increase in IC50 compared to healthy volunteers (HV). The Applicant’s model estimated 
a 30% increase in IC50 in ADA-positive subjects with an ADA titer of 50.
Table 121. Parameter Estimates of the Final Octreotide Exposure-ApoC-III Model
Source: Applicant’s Response to Information Request (September 10, 2024), Table 4, page 5.
Abbreviations: apo, apolipoprotein; IC50, half maximal inhibitory concentration; Kout, first-order elimination rate
The GOF plots from the final exposure-ApoC-III model are shown in Figure 58 and the pcVPC 
plots are shown in Figure 59.

patients. The removal of ADA status and titer on IC50 from the exposure-ApoC-III model 
significantly increased the objective function value by 129 points (for 2 degrees of freedom

IC50 or the estimated PD model parameters, except for the effect of FCS disease on IC50

14.5.2.2. Exposure-TG Model
The exposure-TG model consisted of an indirect response model with a stimulatory drug effect 
on TG removal (elimination) rate constant (Kout). The drug effect on Kout was described as an 
Emax function with maximum effect (Emax) and a concentration at half maximum effect (EC50). 
BSV terms were included on the Kout, Emax and EC50 parameters. A proportional error model was 
used to describe the RUV of TG concentrations, with separate RUV estimated for patients with 
FCS and subject without FCS (including HV).
Table 122 summarizes the parameter estimates of the final exposure-TG model. The model 
identified disease status as a covariate on Kout and EC50. Patients with HTG or CVD experienced 
an approximate 61% reduction in Kout compared to HV or FCS patients. Patients with FCS have 
619% increase in EC50 compared to HV or patients with HTG or CVD. The Applicant’s model 
estimated an increase in EC50 with an ADA titer above 50.
Table 122. Parameter Estimates of the Final Olezarsen Exposure-TG Model
Source: Applicant’s Response to Information Request (September 10, 2024), Table 5, page 5.
Abbreviations: EC50, concentration at half maximum effect; Kout, first-order elimination rate; TG, triglycerides

plots are shown in Figure 61.

14.5.3. Application of the PK and Exposure-Response 
Models
The PK and exposure-response models were used to perform population simulations to support 
dose selection in patients with FCS. Simulations were performed for the following scenarios:
•
50 mg olezarsen Q4W for 13 doses (364 days) and every month (Q1M) for 12 doses (360 
days).
•
80 mg olezarsen Q4W for 13 doses (364 days) and Q1M for 12 doses (360 days).
Figure 62 shows a trend towards a marginally higher percentage change from baseline (CFB) in 
ApoC-III and TG for the 80 mg compared to the 50 mg doses, with overlapping 95% CIs for the 
percent CFB in TG concentrations.
Figure 63 shows that the PK, ApoC-III, and TG profiles for Q4W and Q1M dosing overlap 
following first dose and at steady-state with a marginal difference in the percent CFB in ApoC-
III and TG concentrations at the end of the dosing interval for the Q1M.

Q4W

Reviewer Comment
The simulations from the PK and exposure-response models were reproducible and suggest that 
the 80 mg olezarsen administered Q4W or Q1M are acceptable and estimated to achieve, at the 
end of the dosing interval (Ctrough,ss), a percent reduction from baseline in ApoC-III of 73.5% for 
Q4W or 71.6% for Q1M, and percent reductions from baseline in TG of 29.4% for Q4W and 
28.6% for Q1M, in patients with FCS. According to the exposure-TG modeling and simulation, 
50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen administered Q4W are estimated to achieve, at steady-state exposure 
and the end of the dosing interval, a median percent reduction from baseline in TG of 23.4 versus 
29.4%, in patients with FCS. Figure 62 shows a trend towards a marginally higher percentage 
CFB in TG for the 80 mg compared to the 50 mg doses, with overlapping 95% CIs for the 
percent CFB in TG concentrations.
14.5.4. Exploratory Exposure-Safety Analyses
14.5.4.1. Exposure-Platelet Count Relationship
The Applicant performed exploratory analysis to evaluate the relationship between the CFB in 
platelet count versus cumulative AUC (cAUC) and Cmax in Trials ISIS 678354-CS3 and ISIS 
678354-CS8, concluding that there were no substantial trends in the observed change from 
baseline (Figure 64) or the maximum decrease from baseline in platelet count across the entire 
range of cumulative AUC or Cmax.

15. Study/Trial Design
15.1. Trial ISIS 678354-CS3
15.1.1. Clinical Study Protocol Synopsis
The following is the Applicant’s protocol synopsis of Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 from protocol 
amendment 9 (August 2, 2023), which was the final version of the protocol prior to the data 
cutoff.
Title
A Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Phase 3 Study of AKCEA-APOCIII-LRX 
Administered Subcutaneously to Patients With Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (FCS)
Landmark Dates
•
First subject Dosed: December 17, 2020
•
Last subject Enrolled: July 11, 2022
•
Last subject, Last Dose: June 20, 2023
•
Last subject, Last Visit: Subjects still in follow-up time at data cut-off
•
Data Cutoff: July 14, 2023
•
Database Lock Date: August 30, 2023
Objectives
Primary
To evaluate the efficacy of olezarsen as compared to placebo on the percent change in fasting TG 
from baseline.
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 291 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
274
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Secondary
To evaluate the efficacy of olezarsen as compared to placebo on the following:
•
Percent change in fasting ApoC-III from baseline
•
Proportion of subjects who achieve ≥40% reduction in fasting TG from baseline
•

•

•

Study Design
This is a multi-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study. Eligible subjects will 
enter an approximately 4-week, but no more than 8-week, screening period that includes an at 
least 2-week diet stabilization/run-in period for subjects not already on a stable diet, and an 
approximately 2-week qualification period. Following qualification, approximately 60 eligible 
subjects will be randomized 1:1 to Cohort A (50 mg) or Cohort B (80 mg) and each cohort 
further randomized 2:1 to receive olezarsen or placebo in a 53-week treatment period.
Subjects in Cohort A will receive 50 mg of olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.5 
mL) for Weeks 1 through 49 of the treatment period. Subjects in Cohort B will receive 80 mg 
olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.8 mL) for Weeks 1 through 49 of the 
treatment period. Randomization will be stratified by: (1) prior history of pancreatitis (within 10 
years prior to screening); and (2) previous treatment with volanesorsen. Dietary counseling will 
commence at the start of the diet stabilization period and will be reinforced at intervals 
throughout the treatment and follow-up period. Following the Week 53 visit, eligible subjects 
may elect to enroll in an open-label extension (OLE) study pending study approval by the 
institutional review board/independent ethics committee and the appropriate regulatory authority. 
Subjects not participating in the OLE will enter the 13-week post-treatment evaluation period. 
The primary endpoint for the study will be evaluated after the last subject has completed the 
Week 53/ET visit and will be based on the percent change in fasting TG from baseline at the 
primary analysis time point (Month 6).
The subjects, investigators, study staff, and the Applicant are to remain blinded to lipid data 
(central and local) starting after the first dose on Day 1 then throughout the remainder of the 
study.

Population
Inclusion Criteria
1. Must have given written informed consent (signed and dated) and any authorizations required 
by local law and be able to comply with all study requirements
2. Aged ≥18 years at the time of informed consent
3. A diagnosis of Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (type 1 Hyperlipoproteinemia) by 
documentation of confirmed homozygote, compound heterozygote or double heterozygote 
for loss-of-function mutations in type 1-causing genes (such as LPL, GPIHBP1, ApoA5, 
ApoC2, GPD1, or LMF1)
4. Fasting TG ≥880 mg/dL (10 mmol/L) at screening. If fasting TG is <880 mg/dL up to two 
additional tests may be performed with any single test used to qualify
5. History of pancreatitis (defined as a recorded diagnosis of AP or hospitalization or 
emergency room (ER) visit for severe abdominal pain consistent with AP and for which no 
alternate diagnosis was made within 10 years prior to screening. Subjects without a recorded 
history of pancreatitis, or no recorded history within 10 years prior to screening, are also 
eligible but their enrollment will be capped at 35% (i.e., ≤21 of the 60 planned subjects)
6. Willing to follow a diet comprising ≤20 g fat per day during the study
7. Willing to complete all patient-reported outcome assessments throughout the study
8. Satisfy the following:
Females: must be non-pregnant and non-lactating and either:
Surgically sterile (e.g., tubal occlusion, hysterectomy, bilateral salpingectomy, bilateral 
oophorectomy);
Postmenopausal (defined as 12 months of spontaneous amenorrhea in females >55 years 
of age or, in females ≤55 years, 12 months of spontaneous amenorrhea without an 
alternative medical cause and FSH levels in the postmenopausal range for the 
laboratory involved);
Abstinent*; or
If engaged in sexual relations of child-bearing potential, agree to use a highly effective 
contraceptive method from the time of signing the informed consent form until at 
least 17 weeks after the last dose of study drug (olezarsen or placebo)
Males: Surgically sterile, abstinent*, or if engaged in sexual relations with a female of child-
bearing potential, subject is utilizing a highly effective contraceptive method from the 
time of signing the informed consent form until at least 17 weeks after the last dose of 
study drug (olezarsen or placebo)
* Abstinence is only acceptable as true abstinence, i.e., when this is in line with the preferred 
and usual lifestyle of the subject. Periodic abstinence (e.g., calendar, ovulation, symptothermal, 
post-ovulation methods), declaration of abstinence for the duration of a trial and withdrawal are 
not acceptable methods of contraception
9. Atypical antipsychotic medications (e.g., olanzapine and clozapine) will be allowed if on a 
stable dose for at least 3 months prior to screening and dose and regimen expected to remain 
constant through the end of their participation in this study
10. The following concomitant medications will be allowed if on a stable dose for at least 4 
weeks prior to screening and dose and regimen expected to remain constant through the end

Statistical Plan
Sample Size Considerations
Based upon prior clinical trial experience with FCS subjects, the SD of the percent change from 
baseline in TGs is approximately 46%. With 14 subjects in each olezarsen treatment group and 
14 in the pooled placebo group, there would be a 90% power to detect a 60% difference between 
each olezarsen treatment group and pooled placebo group at an alpha level of 0.05 (two-sided), 
assuming 60% reduction in the olezarsen treatment subjects and no change in the placebo 
subjects. Approximately 60 subjects will be enrolled in this trial to account for potential early 
dropouts and to facilitate safety evaluations.
Eligible subjects will be randomized 1:1 to Cohort A or Cohort B and each cohort further 
randomized 2:1 (olezarsen: placebo) and stratified for:
•
Prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to screening* vs. no history of pancreatitis 
or no history within 10 years prior to screening
•
Previous treatment with volanesorsen
* History of pancreatitis is defined as a recorded diagnosis of AP or hospitalization or ER visit 
for severe abdominal pain consistent with AP and for which no alternate diagnosis was made
Analysis of the Primary Endpoint
The primary analysis of the primary endpoint is to compare the percent CFB to the primary 
analysis time point in fasting TG between each olezarsen treatment group vs. pooled placebo 
using an analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) model in the full analysis set (FAS). The ANCOVA 
model will include the effects of treatment (olezarsen 80 mg, olezarsen 50 mg, or placebo), two 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 299 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
282
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
randomization stratification factors (history of pancreatitis and previous treatment with 
volanesorsen), and log-transformed baseline TG. To account for potential heterogeneity in 
variance, the 95% CI of the treatment differences will be calculated using the robust variance 
estimator based on the Bell and McCaffrey method (Imbens and Kolesar 2016). If subjects have 
intercurrent event(s) before Week 23, e.g., treatment discontinuation, use of additional 
medication, changes in background or concomitant treatments, the treatment policy strategy will 
be implemented, the subjects’ assessments post intercurrent events will continue to be collected 
and utilized in the primary analysis. Subjects with a missing TG value after discontinuation will 
have TG value multiply imputed at primary analysis time point based on the known assessments 
from the retrieved dropouts (i.e., subjects who discontinue before Week 23 but still have TG 
assessments at Week 23, 25, or 27), if there is a sufficient number of retrieved dropouts. The 
imputation model will include the following variables: TG values at baseline and postbaseline 
visits and the two randomization stratification factors. The multiple imputation will be stratified 
by treatment group. If there is not a sufficient number of retrieved dropouts, missing TG value at 
the primary time point will be imputed using a “wash-out” (Jump to Reference, J2R) multiple 
imputation approach. With the approach, subjects treated with olezarsen who discontinue study 
will have missing post-discontinuation TG values multiply imputed based on the fasting TG at 
baseline, two randomization stratification factors, and the imputation model for the placebo 
group. Subjects in the placebo group who discontinue the study will have missing post-
discontinuation TG values multiply imputed based on fasting TG values at baseline and 
postbaseline visits from the placebo group.
It is anticipated that the rate of missing data is low.
The null hypothesis of the primary analysis is that the percent CFB to primary analysis time 
point in fasting TG is the same between each olezarsen and placebo treatment groups. The 
alternative hypothesis is that the percent CFB to primary analysis time point in fasting TG is 
different between each olezarsen and placebo treatment groups.
The primary efficacy analysis will take place after the last subject has completed the Week 
53/ET visit and the database has been locked, and will be based on the percent CFB in fasting 
TG at primary analysis time point (end of Month 6). Details of the controlled access to the 
unblinded data will be outlined in the statistical analysis plan (SAP) and unblinding plan.
During the database lock cleaning process, redacted source documentation may need to be shared 
securely with the site Clinical Research Associate for source data verification procedures The 
following sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint will be conducted to assess the robustness 
of the primary analysis result, and details of the analyses will be outlined in the SAP:
•
The primary analysis described above will be repeated in the per-protocol set (PPS).
•
The primary analysis will be repeated in the subset of FAS with no missing TG value at the 
baseline and primary analysis time point.
•
Additional sensitivity analyses using controlled imputations (pattern mixture models) with 
ANCOVA will be conducted in the FAS to assess the robustness of the primary analysis 
results.
–
For subjects treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study due to adverse event (AE) 
or lack of efficacy (i.e., informative missing), their missing TG data after treatment 
discontinuation will be multiply imputed using the copy increment from reference (CIR) 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 300 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
283
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
approach based on the estimates from of the placebo subjects. The CIR approach is 
detailed in (Carpenter et al. 2013). The assumption is when a subject discontinues 
treatment due to informative missing, from withdrawal onwards they would progress in 
the same way as the subjects in the placebo group. For subjects who discontinue due to 
other reasons and all subjects from the placebo group, their missing data will be treated as 
non-informative missing, and data will be multiply imputed based on the distribution of 
their own arm.
–
All subjects treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study will have missing post-
treatment discontinuation TG values multiply imputed using the CIR approach based on 
the estimates from the placebo subjects. Missing TG for placebo subjects will be imputed 
based on the distribution of the placebo group.
•
A nonparametric Wilcoxon rank-sum test will be performed on the percent change in fasting 
TG from baseline to the primary analysis time point in the FAS. Missing data will be handled 
using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis.
•
An additional sensitivity analysis using the two-dimensional tipping-point approach will be 
conducted in the FAS to assess the impact of missing data on the result of the primary 
analysis. A range of penalties will be added to the imputed values for each group until the 
result becomes non-significant and tipping points are identified.
•
An ANCOVA model will be performed in the FAS using the nature log transformed data. 
Missing data will be handled using the same multiple imputation method as the primary 
analysis.
Analysis of Secondary Endpoints
•
The percent change in fasting TG from baseline to Month 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in 
the same way as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint.
•
The proportion of subjects who achieve ≥40% reduction in fasting TG from baseline to the 
primary analysis time point in the FAS will be compared between each olezarsen treatment 
group and placebo group using a logistic regression model with the treatment group, history 
of pancreatitis (yes/no), previous treatment with volanesorsen (yes/no) as the factors, and 
log-transformed baseline TG as a covariate. Missing TG values will be imputed using the 
same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. A 
sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the FAS, in which all subjects who have missing 
data and discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.
•
The percent change in fasting ApoC-III, ApoB-48, and non-HDL-C from baseline to the 
primary analysis time point and Month 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in the same way as the 
primary analysis of the primary endpoint.
•
The proportion of subjects who achieve fasting TG ≤880 mg/dL at the primary analysis time 
point will be analyzed in the same way as the proportion of subjects who achieve ≥40% 
reduction in fasting TG. Missing values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation 
method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. It will be conducted in the subset of 
FAS with baseline TG >880 mg/dL. A sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the subset of

FAS with baseline TG >880 mg/dL, in which all subjects who have missing data and

data and discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.
•
The proportion of subjects who achieve fasting TG ≤500 mg/dL at the primary analysis time 
point will be analyzed in the same way as the proportion of subjects who achieve ≥40% 
reduction in fasting TG. Missing values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation 
method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. It will be conducted in the subset of 
FAS with baseline TG >500 mg/dL. A sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the subset of 
FAS with baseline TG >500 mg/dL, in which all subjects who have missing data and 
discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.

screening period (that may be extended to 8 weeks if assessments cannot be completed within 
the 4-week period) that includes an at least 2-week diet/life-style stabilization/run-in period, and 
an approximately 2-week qualification period. Following qualification, approximately 152 
eligible patients will be randomized 1:1 to Cohort A (50 mg) or Cohort B (80 mg) and each 
cohort further randomized 3:1 to receive olezarsen or placebo in a 53-week treatment period.

mL) during Weeks 1-49 of the treatment period. Patients in Cohort B will receive 80 mg 
olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.8 mL) during Weeks 1-49 of the treatment 
period. The 80 mg dose (blinded olezarsen or placebo) may be adjusted to 50 mg Q4W due to 
tolerability or safety reasons at any point during the study following consultation with the

Statistical Plan
Sample Size Considerations
With 33 patients in each olezarsen treatment group and 22 in the pooled placebo group, there 
would be an approximately 80% power to detect a 60% difference between each olezarsen 
treatment group and pooled placebo group at an alpha level of 0.05 (two-sided), assuming 60% 
reduction in the olezarsen treatment group, no change in the placebo patients, and a common SD 
of approximately 73%. It is estimated that drop-out rate is approximately 20%. Approximately 
152 patients will be enrolled in this trial to account for potential early dropouts and to facilitate 
general safety evaluations.
Analysis of the Primary Endpoint
The primary analysis of the primary endpoint is to compare the percent CFB to Month 6 in 
fasting TG between each olezarsen treatment group vs. pooled placebo group using an 
ANCOVA model in the FAS. The ANCOVA model will include the effects of treatment 
(olezarsen 80 mg, olezarsen 50 mg, or placebo), and log-transformed baseline TG. If patients 
have intercurrent event(s) before Week 25, e.g., treatment discontinuation, use of additional 
medication, changes in background or concomitant treatments, the treatment policy strategy will 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 307 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
290
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
be implemented, the patients’ assessments post intercurrent events will continue to be collected. 
The missing TG value at Months 6 or 12 will be imputed using an imputation model that 
contains the following variables: baseline fasting TG, fasting TG at each postbaseline visit and 
the multiple imputation will be stratified by treatment group (Schafer 1997; Schafer 1999).
The Markov chain Monte Carlo method will be used under the multivariate normality 
assumption to impute the missing values by treatment group. To conform to the multivariate 
normality assumption, baseline and postbaseline TG will be natural log-transformed before the 
imputation process, the data will be back transformed to create the imputed data set after the 
imputation.
The primary efficacy analysis will take place after the last patient has completed the Week 53/ET 
visit and the database has been locked. Details of the controlled access to the unblinded data will 
be outlined in the SAP and unblinding plan.
The following sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint will be conducted to assess the 
robustness of the primary analysis result, and details of the analyses will be outlined in the SAP:
The primary analysis described above will be repeated in the PPS.
•
The primary analysis will be repeated in the subset of FAS with no missing TG value at 
baseline and Month 6.
•
A nonparametric Wilcoxon rank-sum test will be performed on the percent change in TG 
from baseline to Month 6. Missing data will be handled using the same multiple imputation 
method as the primary analysis. The treatment difference will be assessed using the Hodges-
Lehmann estimator of the location shift and corresponding 95% CI. The analysis will be 
conducted in the FAS.
•
A two-dimensional tipping-point analysis will be conducted on the imputed data (based on 
the primary imputation approach as described above) to assess the influence of missingness 
on the conclusion of the treatment difference. A range of penalties will be added to the 
imputed values for each group until the result becomes non-significant and tipping points are 
identified. The analysis will be conducted in the FAS.
•
A few sensitivity analyses using controlled imputations (pattern mixture models [PMM]) 
with ANCOVA similar to that for primary analysis will be conducted in the FAS to assess 
the robustness of the primary analysis results. With these approaches, missing not at random 
is assumed and the mean function for the missing data from patients treated with olezarsen 
who discontinue the study is pre-specified in ways that assess the robustness of the missing at 
random assumption in the primary analysis.
–
Patients with missing TG value at Month 6 will be imputed using a PMM. Specifically, 
for patients treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study due to AE or lack of 
efficacy (i.e., informative missing), their missing TG data after treatment discontinuation 
will be multiply imputed using the CIR approach based on the estimates from of the 
placebo patients. The CIR approach is detailed in (Carpenter et al. 2013). The assumption 
is when a patient discontinues treatment due to informative missing, from discontinuation 
onwards he/she would progress in the same way as the patients in the placebo group. For 
patients who discontinue due to other reasons and all patients from the placebo group, 
their missing data will be treated as non-informative missing, and data will be multiply 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 308 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
291
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
imputed based on the distribution of their own arm. The analysis will be conducted in the 
FAS. Baseline and postbaseline TG will be natural log-transformed before the imputation 
process, the data will be back transformed to create the imputed data set after the 
imputation.
–
All patients treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study will have missing post-
treatment discontinuation TG values multiply imputed using the CIR approach based on 
the estimates from the placebo patients. Missing TG for placebo patients will be imputed 
using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis
–
Patients with missing TG values after treatment discontinuation will have TG values 
multiply imputed based on the known assessments from the retrieved dropouts (e.g., for 
Month 6, the retrieved dropouts include those patients who discontinue treatment before 
Week 25 but still have assessments at Week 25 or 27), if there are a sufficient number of 
retrieved dropouts. The imputation model will include the following variables: fasting TG 
values at baseline and postbaseline visits and the multiple imputation will be stratified by 
treatment group. If there is no sufficient number of retrieved dropouts, missing TG value 
after treatment discontinuation at Month 6 or 12 will be imputed using a “wash-out” 
(Jump to Reference, J2R) multiple imputation approach. With the approach, patients 
treated with olezarsen who discontinue study will have missing post-discontinuation TG 
values multiply imputed based on baseline fasting TG and history of pancreatitis (yes/no) 
of the patient, and the imputation model for the placebo group. Patients in the placebo 
group who discontinue the study will have missing post-treatment discontinuation TG 
values multiply imputed using the myocardial infarction method as the primary analysis. 
Baseline and postbaseline TG will be natural log-transformed before the imputation 
process, the data will be back transformed to create the imputed data set after the 
imputation.
Analysis of Secondary Endpoints
•
The percent change in fasting TG from baseline to Month 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in 
the same way as for the primary endpoint.
•
The proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG <150 mg/dL (1.69 mmol/L) at Month 6 
and proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG <150 mg/dL at Month 12 in the subset of 
FAS with baseline TG <500 mg/dL will be compared between each olezarsen treatment 
group and pooled placebo group using a logistic regression model with the treatment group 
as the factor, and log-transformed baseline TG as a covariate. Missing TG values will be 
imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary 
endpoint. The analysis will be repeated in the subset of PPS with baseline TG <500 mg/dL as 
a sensitivity analysis. An additional sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the subset of 
FAS with baseline TG <500 mg/dL, in which all patients who have missing data due to 
discontinuation will be considered as non-responders.
•
The proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG <500 mg/dL at Month 6 and proportion of 
patients who achieve fasting TG <500 mg/dL at Month 12 will be analyzed in the same way 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 309 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
292
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
as the proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG <150 mg/dL. It will be conducted in the 
subset of FAS or PPS with baseline TG ≥500 mg/dL.
•
The percent change in fasting ApoC-III, VLDL-C, remnant cholesterol, non-HDL-C, HDL-
C, TC, ApoB, LDL-C, and ApoA-1 from baseline to Months 6 and 12 in the FAS will be 
analyzed in the same way as for the primary endpoint.
•
The adjudicated AP event rates in the subset of FAS with two or more events of adjudicated 
AP in 5 years prior to enrollment and in the FAS during the treatment period (Week 1 
through Week 53/ET) will be compared between pooled olezarsen treatment and placebo 
group using a negative binomial regression model or Poisson regression model with the 
treatment group and log transformed baseline TG as the factors, and number of adjudicated 
AP events in 5 years prior to the enrollment as a covariate. The logarithm of time in year that 
each patient observed during the treatment period will be used as an offset variable. In the 
case of not enough events to perform the model-based analysis, the number and percentage 
of subjects along with the two-sided 95% exact CI using Clopper-Pearson methodology will 
be summarized based on FAS. Fisher’s exact p-value will also be provided.

Statistical Plan
Treatment duration and amount of olezarsen received will be summarized by treatment group in 
the index study and overall. The proportion of patients who experience the events listed in the 
endpoints above from baseline to Week 53, 105, and 157 and during the study period will be 
tabulated by treatment group in the index study and overall.
For the lab events, the proportion of patients based on the confirmed results will also be 
tabulated.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Patient incidence rates of all AEs will be tabulated by MedDRA system organ class (SOC) and 
PT, and by treatment group in the index study and overall. Tables and/or narratives of treatment 
emergent deaths, serious and significant AEs, including early withdrawals due to AEs, will also 
be provided.
All TEAEs, all TEAEs potentially related to olezarsen, all serious TEAEs, and all serious TEAEs 
potentially related to olezarsen will be summarized. Laboratory tests to ensure patient safety, 
including chemistry panel, complete blood count with differential, coagulation panel, etc., will 
be summarized by study visit for each treatment group in the index study and overall. These 
safety variables will also be presented as change and percent CFB over time, as appropriate.
Vital sign and ECG measures will be tabulated.
The adjudicated MACE event rates will be summarized by the treatment group in index study 
and overall.
The change and percent change in fasting TG and other PD endpoints (ApoC-III, ApoB, VLDL-
C, etc.) from baseline at Week 53, Week 105, and Week 157 will be summarized.
The adjudicated AP event rates (Week 1 through Weeks: 53, 105, and 157) will be summarized.
16. Efficacy
16.1. Trial ISIS 678354-CS3, Subgroup Analyses
Subgroup Analyses of the Primary Endpoint for Study CS3
Subgroup analyses were performed on the primary endpoint, fasting TG by age group (<65 
years, ≥65 years), sex (female, male), region (outside of USA, USA), race (Asian, Native 
Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander, Other, White), and ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, Not 
Hispanic or Latino). Figure 67 shows the subgroup analysis results for olezarsen 50 mg vs. 
placebo, and Figure 68 shows the subgroup analysis results for olezarsen 80 mg vs. placebo. The 
subgroup analyses were performed using the FAS population. This study was conducted in 
mainly White, females, <65 years of age, outside of the USA. Due to some very small patient 
subgroups, some estimates/95% CIs were not able to be calculated. The subgroup for race was 
combined to Other and White due to very few patients in the Asian, Native Hawaiian and Other 
Pacific Islander, and Other categories.

There were likely some random highs and random lows in sample estimates of subgroup 
treatment effects due to small sample size and large variability for some subgroups. Therefore, 
we also derived shrinkage estimates of subgroup treatment effects using a Bayesian hierarchical 
model based on summary sample estimates. The total variability in the sample estimates is the 
sum of the within subgroup variability of the sample estimator and the across subgroups 
variability in underlying/true parameter values. A shrinkage estimate of the subgroup treatment 
effect, which borrows information from the other subgroups while estimating the treatment effect 
for a specific subgroup, is a “weighted” average of the sample estimate and overall estimate. The 
weights are based on the ratio of the between subgroup variability to the within subgroup 
variability. The greater that ratio the smaller the weight on the overall estimate (the less the 
shrinkage). We used the same flat prior to derive shrinkage estimates for all subgroups (age, sex, 
Reference ID: 5499859

===== PAGE 324 =====
NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
307
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
region, race, and ethnicity). The Bayesian hierarchical model assumptions for fasting TG at 
Month 6 are:
For i = 1, 2… Yi represents the observed sample estimate of treatment effect in a subgroup level 
i, assume Yi~N(μi, σi2) where 
•
σi2 are the observed variance for sample estimates 
•
μi ~ N(μ, τ2) 
•
μ ~ N(0, 140), 1/τ2 ~ Gamma(0.001, 0.001) 
The variance should be selected relative to the residual standard deviation from the primary 
analysis model. In general, it should be 4 to 10 times of the residual SD. A flat prior with mean 0 
and SD of 140 was chosen. This SD is 4 times the residual SD and so this assumption would not 
be influential. Figure 69 for olezarsen 50 mg vs. placebo and Figure 70 for olezarsen 80 mg vs. 
placebo compares the conventional subgroup analysis results of the sample estimate (in blue) and 
Bayesian shrinkage estimate (in red) for the endpoint of fasting TG at Month 6. The overall 
treatment effect for 50 mg was -21.5 (95% CI: -48.2, 5.3) and for 80 mg was -44.5 (95% CI: -
72.0, -17.0). Subgroup analysis using Bayesian shrinkage estimate exhibits narrower CI, and the 
shrinkage subgroup estimate is closer to the overall mean.

from the exit interview substudy indicated that a total of 14 of the 18 patients (78%) reported

substudy were not conclusive with improvements in FCS symptoms and impacts (median change 
was generally 0 in the FCS-SIS measure). As such, it is difficult to determine whether any 
treatment benefits were observed as they relate to FCS symptoms and impacts.

COAs and exit interview substudy.

Supplementary Safety Review–Changes in Other Hematopoietic Parameters
Issue
Higher proportions of olezarsen 80 mg treated subjects compared to placebo-treated subjects 
reported an event in CS3 using the FMQ (narrow) search terms ‘anemia’ and ‘leukopenia’ and 
‘anemia’ (both 50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen compared to placebo) in CS8.   
Background
Because of the reduction in platelet count observed with olezarsen treatment, there was concern 
that other hematopoietic parameters may be adversely affected. 
Assessment
Higher proportions of olezarsen 80 mg treated subjects compared to placebo-treated subjects 
reported an event in CS3 using the FMQ (narrow) search terms ‘anemia’ and ‘leukopenia’ and 
‘anemia’ (both 50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen compared to placebo) in CS8.

In CS3, adverse shifts in Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event toxicity grades of ≥2 
severity categories in hemoglobin occurred in three olezarsen-treated subjects and no placebo-
treated subjects10. One of the three olezarsen-treated subjects (CS3-
) experienced a 
shift in hemoglobin from within reference range to a ‘severe’ category. This subject is described 
below:
•
Subject CS3-
 was treated with olezarsen 50 mg, experienced a reduction in 
hemoglobin from 12.3 g/dL (within reference range) at baseline to a nadir of 7.7 g/dL 
(severe) on Study Day 150. The subject had voluntarily withdrawn from the Trial CS3 on 
Study Day 114 (last dose of olezarsen Study Day 90) – she had recently been hospitalized for 
an episode of pancreatitis (Study Day 102). On the same day as the low hemoglobin (Study 
Day 150), the subject presented to the emergency department with chest pain. During the 
examination her heart rate was registered at 180 bpm. No abnormal troponin was reported. 
An ECG was performed which showed Atrioventricular Nodal Reentrant Tachycardia. She 
received 5 mg IV of adenosine. Her heart rate returned to sinus rhythm, and she was 
discharged. The subject’s hemoglobin improved at the next study visit (also last visit – 
follow-up week 13, Study Day 189) to 9.9 g/dL (moderate) and ECG was normal.

g/dL; Severe=hemoglobin <8.0 g/dL

Supplementary Information for Key Safety Review Issue–Immunogenicity
Subjects with a positive ADA status could be further classified into different ADA types based 
on their baseline ADA status and change in ADA titer post-treatment. These definitions are 
described in Section 17.
Treatment-Emergent ADA
Subjects with either treatment-induced ADA or treatment-boosted ADA as described below:
•
Treatment-induced ADA: ADA developed de novo (seroconversion) following study drug 
administration (i.e., formation of ADA any time after the initial drug administration in a 
subject without pre-existing ADA, i.e., baseline negative ADA).
•
Treatment-boosted ADA: pre-existing ADA that were boosted to a higher level following 
study drug administration (i.e., any time after the initial drug administration the ADA titer is 
greater than the baseline titer by a factor of 8-fold or more).
Treatment-Unaffected
ADA: pre-existing ADA that were not affected (boosted) following study drug administration 
(i.e., any time after the initial drug administration the ADA titer is 4-fold or less).
Subjects with positive ADA status that was treatment-induced were also classified as being 
‘transient’ or ‘persistent’ based on the following definitions.
Transient ADA response: 
•
Treatment-induced ADA detected only at one sampling time point during the treatment or 
follow-up observation period (excluding the last sampling time point, which will be 
considered persistent unless shown to be undetectable at a later time) or 
•
Treatment-induced ADA detected at two or more sampling time points during the treatment 
(including follow-up period if any), where the first and last ADA-positive samples 
(irrespective of any negative samples in between) are separated by a period less than 16 
weeks, and the subject’s last sampling time point is ADA-negative.
Persistent ADA response:
•
Treatment-induced ADA detected at two or more sampling time points during the treatment 
(including follow-up period if any), where the first and last ADA-positive samples 
Reference ID: 5499859

NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reviewer Comment
Discussion with the non-clinical review team regarding this case noted that in the fertility studies 
of both olezarsen and volanesorsen, drug treated males were mated with drug-treated females, so 
sex specificity of effects cannot be ascertained. However, there were no effects on fetal 
development reported with olezarsen. For volanesorsen, there were increases in rib variations 
(not statistically significant) noted in the offspring, but nothing analogous to the muscle spasms 
or eye issues reported clinically. In the pre-/postnatal toxicity study with volanesorsen in which 
pregnant females were treated throughout gestation and lactation, there were reductions in 
maternal body weights which resulted in lower pup weights at the HD of 25 mg/kg 
(approximately 25x the clinical dose based on BSA). The reduced pup weights in turn 
contributed to slight delays in the occurrence of various developmental landmarks. Delays of this 
nature are not unusual when there are effects on pup body weights.  
Tissue distribution studies provide further support for the effect not being related to olezarsen.  
In the rat, testicular tissue levels averaged 0.2% of a single SC dose of 5 mg/kg (approximately 
3-fold the clinical dose on a BSA basis). Given the low level in testicular tissue, targeted delivery 
to the liver if any drug in the ejaculate actually made it into the female circulatory system and 
across the placental barrier, it is improbable that treatment of the male partner led to the observed 
anomalies.
There are anecdotal case reports in the literature of women with FCS treated with volanesorsen 
during their pregnancies with successful outcomes for both mother and baby (Wanninayake et al. 
2024).
Autoinjector
The to-be-marketed formulation of olezarsen will be an autoinjector. The autoinjector was 
introduced into the open-label clinical trials, CS7 and CS13. In CS13, at the time of the NDA 
submission there were 32 subjects administering olezarsen with the autoinjector with a mean 
exposure of 99 days (minimum 6 days, maximum 199 days). At the 90-day safety update, there 
were 56 subjects with a mean exposure of 151 days (minimum 28 days, maximum 317 days).
Use of the autoinjector in CS7 occurred after the data cutoff for both the NDA and 90-day safety 
update and therefore no information is available. 
The amount of exposure to the autoinjector is small. Refer to the Human Factors and Center for 
Devices and Radiological Health reviews for acceptability of this device.

22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of 
Scientific Investigations, Other 
Inspections)
Two clinical site inspections and a Contract Research Organization (CRO) inspection were 
completed by the Office of Scientific Investigations. Based on the results of these inspections, 
Trials CS3 and CS8 appear to have been conducted adequately, and the clinical data generated 
by these sites appear to be reliable in support of this NDA.
The clinical sites were chosen using a risk-based approach primarily based on numbers of 
enrolled subjects, treatment effect, protocol deviations, and prior inspection history. The two 
sites included Seth Baum, M.D. (Site # 2138) and Alejandro De La Cruz, M.D. (Site # 3195).
1. Seth Baum, M.D. (Site # 2138)
7900 Glades Road, Suite 400
Boca Raton, FL 33434-4104
Inspection dates: August 5 to 7, 2024
Trials CS3 and CS8
This was the first FDA inspection of Dr. Baum. For Trial CS3, the site screened 9 subjects and 
enrolled 5 subjects. All 5 subjects completed the study. Source records for all 9 screened subjects 
were reviewed. For Trial CS8, the site screened 14 subjects and enrolled 7 subjects. Five subjects 
completed the study, and two subjects discontinued the study: 1 due to AE of GI disorder 
(olezarsen 50 mg group) and 1 due to GI cancer diagnosis (olezarsen 80 mg group). Source 
records for all 14 screened subjects were reviewed.
The inspection reviewed the trials’ protocols and amendments, ICFs and versions, 
documentation of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records, investigational product 
(investigational product) accountability records, both paper and electronic Case Report Forms 
(eCRFs) and electronic data capture (EDC) system, protocol deviations and related regulatory 
documents. The submitted data were verifiable with source records at the study site. The primary 
efficacy endpoint data of the mean percent change in fasting TG from baseline at Month 6 were 
centrally adjudicated and were initially blinded to the site. The inspection verified the results 
with no discrepancies noted. There were no underreporting of AEs or SAEs. In general, the 
Reference ID: 5499859

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
inspection verified adequate source data for the inspected study subjects, with no deficiencies 
reported.
2. Alejandro De La Cruz, M.D. (Site # 3195)
13218 SW 8th Street Miami, FL 33184-1176
Inspection dates: July 8 to 16, 2024
Trial CS8
This was the first FDA inspection of Dr. De La Cruz. For Trial CS8, the site screened 64 subjects 
and enrolled 32 subjects. Except for one subject (olezarsen 50 mg group) who withdrew due to 
AE of ecchymosis at the injection site, 31 subjects completed the study. ICFs for 12/64 screened 
subjects, all source records for 11/32 of the enrolled subjects, and primary efficacy endpoint and 
AEs for all 32 randomized subjects were reviewed.
The inspection reviewed the protocol and amendments, English and Spanish language ICFs and 
versions, documentation of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records, 
investigational product accountability records, paper CRFs with EDC entries and audit trails, 
protocol deviations and related regulatory documents. The submitted data were verifiable with 
source records at the study site. The primary efficacy endpoint data of the mean percent change 
in fasting TG from baseline at Month 6 were verified with no discrepancies noted. There was no 
underreporting of SAEs. The inspection noted several protocol deviations to include three 
subjects that did not meet all inclusion or exclusion criteria and two AEs (“redness at the 
injection site” and “nausea”) that were recorded in source documentation but were not entered 
into the EDC. In general, the inspection verified adequate source data for the inspected subjects, 
with no significant deficiencies noted.
3. Medpace Inc. (CRO)
5400 Medpace Way
Cincinnati, OH 45227-1530
Inspection dates: July 8 to 11, 2024
Trials CS3 and CS8
This was the second FDA inspection of the CRO Medpace. Previous inspection was in February 
2024 with no good clinical practice issues identified. For both trials, Medpace was responsible 
for clinical trial management, study startup and maintenance, subject recruitment and retention, 
clinical monitoring, Data and Safety Monitoring Board meeting organization and attendance; and 
biostatistics analysis for Trial CS8. The inspection reviewed the CRO’s standard operating 
procedures (SOPs), training records, monitoring records, site investigator documents, contracts 
with the Applicant and 3rd party vendors, meeting minutes, non-compliance issues management, 
Data and Safety Monitoring Board meeting documents, and biostatistics data transfer 
verification. Financial disclosure forms and monitoring files were reviewed for four sites for 
Trial CS3 and five sites for Trial CS8 with no good clinical practice issues identified.
In general, the CRO’s management and monitoring of Trials CS3 and CS8 appear adequate, with 
no significant deficiencies noted.

major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI submitted on April 19, 
2024 (Table 136). The PI was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, 
is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and 
scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides 
clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.

● Adverse reactions related to laboratory tests (decrease in 
platelet count, increase in glucose, increase in liver enzymes, 
increase in LDL-cholesterol and total apolipoprotein B) were 
added.
7 DRUG INTERACTIONS
N/A

(e.g., Pregnancy, Lactation, Females 
and Males of Reproductive Potential, 
Pediatric Use, Geriatric Use, Renal

the FDA’s interpretation of the animal data and to clarify the 
origin of these results.
● Section 8.5 Geriatric Use: Revised language for consistency 
with the draft guidance for industry Geriatric Information in

(September 2020).

24. Postmarketing Requirements and 
Commitments
The following postmarketing requirement is recommended:
Complete the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials (ISIS 678354-CS5 
[CORE] and ISIS 678354-CS6 [CORE 2]) and the two-year open-label extension (OLE) trial 
(ISIS 678354-CS15) to evaluate the potential for drug induced liver injury with Tryngolza. 
Interim Report Submission (ISIS 678354-CS5, ISIS 678354-CS6 trial data; OLE 678354-CS15 
trial): June 2026 
Interim Report Submission (OLE 678354-CS15 trial): June 2027
Interim Report Submission (OLE 678354-CS15 trial): June 2028
Trial Completion: December 2028 
Final Report Submission: June 2029  
All three trials should be completed by the trial completion milestone and the final report 
submission should include data from all three trials.
